# SFM FX New Age — Canonical Progress Diary and Notebook

This is the **only** project progress diary, implementation notebook, and cross-agent handoff file.

## Mandatory agent protocol

1. Read this file before modifying the project.
2. Work from dependency order and respect the ownership map below.
3. Do not create another progress, status, handoff, diary, TODO, plan-tracking, or scratch-notes file.
4. Do not rewrite historical entries. Add a new dated entry after verification.
5. Every entry records scope, files changed, commands run, observed results, unfinished work, and the next dependency-safe action.
6. Shared-spine files are coordinator-owned. Tab workers must not edit them unless the diary explicitly transfers ownership.
7. Active product language uses **Issue** for the human action that requests creation of a new pending order.
8. Account class is broker metadata. It is not an application eligibility gate.

## Stable product decisions

- Product: local Debian/X11 Forex signal platform.
- Navigation: four user workspaces and 42 stable backend view identities: 10 / 10 / 13 / 9. The later 2026-11-06 user direction makes Tab 2 a dynamic Trading Tools container and keeps its ten switchboard contracts internal for agents; do not fabricate ten user widgets to preserve the former presentation.
- Execution: one human-initiated pending Limit-order path; no autonomous entry engine.
- Account handling: user selects any cTrader account made available through their authenticated cTrader session.
- Appearance: reuse the supplied SFM FX Calculator 1.4.0 navy/slate palette, DejaVu Sans, compact rounded cards/controls, breathing room and restrained blue/green/red accents; do not introduce a new visual design per tab.
- Source kit: `${AUTHORING_SOURCE_KIT}/SFM_FX_Signal_Platform_Architecture_Kit_v2_2/` remains read-only provenance.
- Active repository: `${PROJECT_ROOT}/`.

## Dependency order

1. Contracts and canonical registries.
2. Priority event router, component registry, projection store.
3. Runtime composition and tab/backend bridges.
4. Four-tab/42-subtab PySide6 shell.
5. Offline vertical slices.
6. cTrader authentication/account/market-data adapter.
7. Scanner DAG, strategies, LogicTiles, signals.
8. Pending-order Issue gateway and reconciliation.
9. Professional views, packaging, deployment.

## Open integration notes

- The cTrader SDK/OAuth/session supervisor, verified TLS, isolated worker, reconciliation, source-owned calendars, tick timeline and backfill now have **real read-only connected acceptance** for the human-selected [private registered application] account and normal Desktop auto-reconnect. Bounded native order/deal-history mechanics remain separately offline-tested; long-duration/revocation/network-loss behavior and any deliberately authorized broker-changing acceptance are separate gates. Private 0700/0600 token persistence is permission-restricted plaintext, not an encrypted vault.
- The 41 host routes and 44-node/130-edge scanner graph remain declared architecture, not 41 finished domains or 44 implemented sensors. Registered primitives execute through the shared cached scheduler; unsupported advanced/external-feed methods remain unavailable. Tab 3 can add source-backed sensors, variables and recipes through its lane-owned backend installation hook.
- LogicTiles now has bounded typed arithmetic/comparison/history/temporal evaluation, pure preview, saved drafts, explicit version/CAS activation, suspension, rollback and analytical-only outputs. The visual editor remains Tab 3 work; a saved/active rule is not execution authority.
- Shared levels/evidence/overlays, variants, confidence/14-gate helpers, candidate binding, risk preflight and isolated causal replay/sweeps/walk-forward primitives are available. Shared source-bound inspectors, signal preflight and replay/chart controls are implemented. Specialized strategy widgets, genuine advanced algorithms, calibrated research and realistic broker-outcome backtesting remain lane work; synthetic acceptance recipes must never be presented as the New Age strategy.
- Source/account isolation and causal correction retention remain controlling. Quote freshness is owner-configurable; one-sided quote updates retain the oldest side's observation time. Variables from a previous source epoch become stale/UNKNOWN, and native reconciliation/continuity blocks unsafe new Issue operations. Native pending inventory is not automatic adoption, and accepted orders never manufacture positions.
- The code's 45% capital-context row remains an UNCONFIGURED placeholder, not an authoritative sizing service or a reason to reopen decisions already settled in later New Age doctrine. Before risk implementation, reconcile the applicable source-owned policy and distinguish per-pair sizing, account-wide net-floating-loss governance, internal live protection and human issuance. Genuine external-volume/depth/COT/calendar feeds and the optional local-exit addon are not silently substituted or enabled. Account class remains transport/display metadata, never eligibility policy. These gaps do not block development against explicit unavailable states.

## Stable parallel-tab API and handoff instructions

Every tab agent must read this whole file, use the existing project environment, and work only in its named lane. The checkpoint does not authorise automatically starting other agents.

Factory integration:

For Tab 2 use `plugin.register_tool(tool_id, title, factory)` instead of the engineering-page factory below. Its lazy factory receives `(api, parent)`; each tool gets a fresh scoped TabApi and closes only that API/subscriptions on its own window close. Keep the ten `2.*` switchboard contracts internal for agents. The following page-factory example applies to Tabs 1/3/4.

```python
from sfm_fx_new_age.tabs.base import TabPlugin

def build_plugin():
    plugin = TabPlugin(tab_id=N)
    plugin.register("N.1", create_widget)
    return plugin

def create_widget(api, model, parent=None):
    # api is scoped TabApi v1; model is SubtabViewModel.
    # Return a QWidget. The shared shell falls back for unregistered subtabs.
    ...
```

- `api.definitions`: exact immutable definitions belonging to that main tab.
- `api.snapshot(subtab_id) -> TabSnapshot`: `subtab_id`, `version`, truthful `status`, recursively immutable `data`. Shared data includes `symbol`, `backend_owner`, `origin`, `scope_id`, `rows` and command `capabilities`; market views also expose causal `context` when a source exists. An AVAILABLE_BARS_ONLY research view is not a strategy/phase result.
- `api.command(subtab_id, command_name, payload=None) -> CommandResult`: validates lane and subtab allowlist, invokes the shared owner, drains events. Unsupported domain operations return NOT_IMPLEMENTED; invalid input returns REJECTED.
- `api.subscribe(subtab_id, callback) -> unsubscribe`: receives immutable dependency-aware events, including shared selection/account/configuration changes, on the core drain thread. `api.subscribe_snapshots(subtab_id, callback, initial=True)` delivers immutable snapshots instead. Unsubscribe on widget disposal; do not touch Qt widgets from a future worker callback. A widget may implement `refresh()` for the shell timer. The shell calls `api.close()` on its own tab consumers during shutdown; closing a consumer never closes shared feeds or services.
- `api.capabilities(subtab_id)` returns immutable entries for every allowed command: IMPLEMENTED, PARTIAL, SNAPSHOT_ONLY or NOT_IMPLEMENTED, with owner, required/optional fields and scope notes. SNAPSHOT_ONLY is not an implemented chart/filter UI. Exact payload types are enforced; booleans, floats or strings cannot masquerade as integer revisions. SELECT_ACCOUNT accepts only an existing connector session `scope_id`; authentication cannot be supplied by a tab payload.
- Never import SQLite or broker SDKs in a tab. Do not create another router, feed, variable registry, parameter registry, order gateway or rule engine. Never infer account eligibility from account type.
- Reuse `sfm_fx_new_age.ui.theme` and the shared selector/controller context. Do not alter tab counts, IDs or canonical titles. At compact width the full-name selector replaces the crowded nested strip without changing the registry.
- The Issue command is `api.command("4.1", "ISSUE", {"signal_id": ..., "revision": ..., "user_initiated": True, "acknowledge_research": ...})`, only after a deliberate UI click. Local CANCEL/CANCEL_ALL_UNISSUED never cancel broker orders. Broker cancellation is separately named on subtab 4.3.

Dependency-safe lane assignments:

| Lane | First independent work | Consume, do not duplicate |
|---|---|---|
| Tab 1 | Watchlist, linked W1/D1/H4 chart widgets, optional H1, overlays, annotations and replay presentation | Shared bars/levels/overlays/geometry/replay datasets; no parallel calculation/feed |
| Tab 2 | User-facing embeddable tools via `register_tool`; calculator refinement and additional source-backed widgets; switchboard remains internal | Per-open scoped TabApi, shared data/services; pure offline estimates remain explicit and never become orders or fabricated feed values |
| Tab 3 | Source-backed sensor/recipe algorithms plus strategy, qualification, LogicTiles and research widgets | Headless analytical installation API, immutable shared objects, one cached scheduler/rule engine/replay service |
| Tab 4 | Qualified-variant binding, queue/preflight/Issue, cancellation, accounts/native inventory/history/positions | Core-owned candidate binding and the one order/connector gateway; no direct SDK use |
| Coordinator | Shared-contract changes, cross-lane integration and later explicitly authorized connected acceptance | Maintain additive TabApi v1 compatibility and this sole diary; serialize shared edits |

Each lane verifies its tests plus `./scripts/run_checks.sh`, returns exact changed paths and observed results, and stops at its assigned boundary. The coordinator then verifies and updates this file; no separate worker report file is required.

#### Concrete defects and incomplete integrations found by static trace

1. **Tab 4 Cancel buttons do not match the strict payload contract.** `app.py:133-140` starts every action with `user_initiated=True` and adds `signal_id`/`revision` for a selected row. `contracts/commands.py:17-18,52-58` accepts only `signal_id` for CANCEL_SIGNAL and no fields for CANCEL_ALL_UNISSUED. Static set comparison proves unexpected fields, so both visible button routes are rejected by validation. Backend API cancellation tests use the correct payloads (`tests/test_order_flow.py:64-84`); the actual GUI test clicks only Issue (`tests/test_gui_checkpoint.py:13-39`). Repair action-specific payloads and add real button-click assertions; do not weaken schemas.
2. **Switches currently persist intent only.** `core/runtime.py:153-163` saves optional_h1/consumer records. AST call/reference census finds no production consumer readback; `snapshot():209-220` never applies the H1 flag, and no scheduler/subscription consumes the consumer row. `tests/test_tab_checkpoint.py:54-57` protects reconciliation but does not prove the requested analysis/display consumer actually pauses. Add owner-bound consumer lifecycle, visible effective-versus-requested state and behavioral tests.
3. **ATR is shared code, not yet one cached shared computation.** `core/runtime.py:209-212` calls `MarketService.context()` from every eligible snapshot; `services/market.py:122-148` reads all scoped bars and reruns Wilder recurrence. `app.py:273-276` polls the active view every 250ms. Add a revision/source/profile-keyed completed-ATR checkpoint/cache and dirty-forward repair; prove display refresh and alternate tabs do not recalculate it. Full-table JSON scans also need indexed time-series retrieval/pagination before long histories.
4. **Preview and save are conflated.** `core/runtime.py:148-152` calls `save_rule()` for both actions; `services/catalogues.py:67-71` writes the rule on preview. This violates the pure-preview requirement (K docs/04:22-27). Rule save overwrites the current row rather than maintaining versioned active/draft histories. Separate validate/preview/save/activate with no-write preview tests.
5. **Registries/candidate contracts are foundations, not complete semantics.** `services/catalogues.py:48-71` has current key/value/version/unit/freshness records but lacks producer-owned typed definitions, Mutable Surface Index, scoped inheritance, type/bounds validation, coherent group commits and immutable parameter/rule histories. `contracts/domain.py:185-216` lacks recipe/method version, evidence roots, zone/variant identity, confidence/gate/target/parameter lineage; `SignalService.propose():19-34` also requires selected-account binding and units. Extend with source-owned account-independent market-candidate identity plus separate account-specific execution binding, preserving source/account isolation rather than removing it.
6. **Scanner scheduling and publication remain missing.** `core/runtime.py:282-287` marks all 44 declared nodes NOT_IMPLEMENTED. Production AST call census reaches `scanner_graph()` only from checks/helper code; dirty-descendant calculation is not a running scheduler. No real level/evidence/zone/candidate/OSQL pipeline or source-parameter invalidation fanout exists. `SignalService.expire()` is called from OrderService only when Issue finds an expired signal; there is no ongoing expiry/revocation scheduler. Duplicate protection is signal/account-specific, not yet stable setup/variant/evidence dedup across arbitrary signal IDs.
7. **Connector concurrency and restart readiness require a real boundary.** `OrderService.issue():145-151` calls transport.submit synchronously on its caller; `ApplicationRuntime.emit():103-107` drains callbacks on the invoking thread, while `router.py:56-84` does not assert a core-thread identity. The real connector worker/core event pump, Qt marshaling, lifecycle, timeout/cancel callbacks and bounded handoff are not implemented. Normal CLI starts no independent backend service (`__main__.py:36-47`), and window/app close shuts its runtime. Before claiming headless continuity, design/verify GUI attachment versus headless rule/feed lifetime. Startup converts inflight states to UNKNOWN but has no complete native account/order/position reconciliation readiness barrier for new Issue operations on other signals.
8. **Risk preview and broker history are not their own finished services.** `snapshot():255-267` gives 4.2 the same signal rows as 4.1, and 4.8 the same local order store used by correlation. `core/runtime.py:139-144` does not fetch native deal/order history without a specific reconciliation call. `BrokerOutcome` and OrderService native state handling have no fill quantities/deal ledger; no external native-order inventory/adoption or multi-deal chronology is implemented. 4.6 is validated snapshot ingestion, not connected position monitoring.

#### Work ownership and dependency order

- **Coordinator/shared foundations first:** repair payload/preview/control gaps; agree typed chart/level/overlay, registry, recipe/candidate/variant, risk-preview, history, replay and connector schemas; implement the one connector/event-pump plus calendar/gap/backfill services and the one dirty-key scheduler. Do not let tab workers create competing feeds, storage, rule engines, ATR calculations or order gateways.
- **Four independent tab-consumer lanes can start now:** real widgets, navigation, selection, filtering, disabled/unavailable states and pure rendering against supplied typed fixtures through TabApi. Their advanced domain outputs are dependency-gated, not independently reimplemented in UI modules. Keep the coordinator-owned/shared-source rules above.
- **Tab 1 delivery order:** watchlist and real linked W1/D1/H4 chart foundation; optional H1 controls; structural/ATR/overlay consumers once their shared outputs exist; shared replay and native read-only order/position rendering.
- **Tab 2 delivery order:** honest health/capabilities and actual consumer controls; source/account/metadata/freshness/backfill surfaces; typed global registries/MSI; route telemetry/storage diagnostics and service controls.
- **Tab 3 delivery order (largest analytical workstream):** OHLC/range/extreme/swing primitives and causal D1 campaign; structural sensors and level/evidence state; recipe/candidate composition and ATR/targets; evidence clusters/confidence/OSQL; typed LogicTiles/parameter lifecycle; research replay/walk-forward and historical candidate UI. Optional Elliott/harmonics and genuine external-feed sensors remain explicitly unavailable until separately implemented/data-supported.
- **Additional sensor capability work:** TPO/intrabar coverage, volume profiles/VWAP only with suitable weighted observations, depth/footprint only with genuine eligible book/print feeds, COT release-time mapping and calendar feeds remain unimplemented and source-capability-gated. Tick activity must not be relabelled traded volume, global FX order flow or executed buy/sell delta. These shared producers feed the Tab 2 status and Tab 3 analytics/Tab 1 overlays, not separate per-tab calculations.
- **Tab 4 delivery order:** actual Cancel payload regression; selected revision-bound read-only preview and queue lifecycle; known-account/OAuth UI + connector session truth; real pending/native-order/deal/reconciliation/position views. Capital context stays typed UNCONFIGURED where definitions are open. The local exit addon remains optional and separate.
- **Human/external waits are narrow:** actual app setup/approval if needed, user OAuth and authenticated readback, and any deliberately approved connected order test. Missing credentials do not block writing the adapter, any tab UI, analytical contracts, deterministic tests or packaging. Account type remains metadata, never a software eligibility restriction. No order smoke test is authorized by this analysis request.
- **Not mandatory signal-core expansion:** autonomous portfolio entry, reserve recycling/redistribution, mirror execution and optional virtual exits are not pulled into the four-tab signal platform merely because predecessor theory discusses them. Preserve O01–O20 as explicit typed research/unknown policies until resolved; do not import numerical defaults from another SFM program.

### 2026-11-05 — Parallel-tab shared-contract release accepted

**Disposition: READY for four independent tab implementation lanes, not a completed trading product.** This entry supersedes the earlier HOLD and analysis-only dispositions for current work. No subagents/tab workers were started. The controlling shared source is bound below; this remains the only diary.

#### Shared gaps closed

- Fixed actual Cancel/Cancel-all Qt payloads without weakening their schemas; implemented effective registered display/H1, scanner, pair and rule consumers, while protecting physical feeds/reconciliation.
- Separated pure preview, save and activation; added typed owner-bound parameter/variable/surface registries, atomic parameter/configuration CAS, immutable histories, bounded three-valued arithmetic/history/temporal rules, suspension and rollback. Rule outputs remain analytical only. Pair/timeframe variables carry source epochs and freshness; old-epoch or expired observations evaluate UNKNOWN.
- Added cached completed-W1 ATR checkpoints, indexed causal market/analytical lookups and a dependency-ordered, bounded scanner scheduler. Unsupported methods retain explicit unavailable/blocked states. Added immutable lineage-bearing levels/evidence/overlays, source-backed recipes, four buffer variants, source-attested confidence and explicit 14-gate qualification helpers.
- Added deterministic source-isolated replay/run/sweep/walk-forward primitives, provider-owned timezone/session calendars, continuity/gap reporting, bounded backfill ownership and canonical BID tick construction for H1/H4/D1/W1. Initial partial intervals, missing observations and traded volume are never invented.
- Implemented the optional cTrader connector boundary: human OAuth/callback and private token persistence, refresh, endpoint/account discovery without silent selection, hostname-verifying TLS, isolated SDK/Twisted process, bounded request/event handoff, native specifications/quotes/trendbars, partial/cumulative-fill handling, idempotent native activity and periodic reconciliation. Authentication alone never means new-Issue readiness; stale reconciliation, unresolved readback, source gaps and UNKNOWN orders remain blockers.
- Added independent bid/ask observation times and sequence-aware one-sided quote updates, same-clock native snapshot sequencing, disconnect revocation, redacted malformed-frame recovery and request backpressure. Native pending inventory remains read-only evidence, not local-order adoption or an inferred position.
- Added bounded native order/deal history requests with account-scoped durable job/record data, time-window bisection and exact deduplication. `hasMore`, same-millisecond overflow, page/concurrency budgets, timeouts/errors and interruption stay visibly incomplete rather than falsely COMPLETE. Broker-provider behavior remains subject to connected acceptance.
- Completed immutable named read adapters for **95 dataset bindings across all 42 subtabs**. Pagination hashes bind source/account/query identity; response extras are bounded too. Historical cutoffs are supported only on causal datasets; current-only state rejects pseudo-historical reads. Display-disabled views do not leak forming-bar extras.
- Added headless analytical plugin installation and core-owned **human-only research-candidate → local signal binding**. The binder checks current candidate/recipe/evidence/zone lineage, all 14 known-passing gates, variant blockers, source epoch, revision and configuration. Binding sends nothing; pure preflight is repeated immediately before a separately deliberate Issue. Recipe deactivation, stale evaluation or UNKNOWN qualification cannot be acknowledged away.
- Corrected the shared footer to report current connector state instead of always saying disconnected. Generic panels remain a fallback, not professional completed tab widgets.

#### Stable lane interfaces — controlling instructions

- Widgets continue in `tabs/tabN/**` and `tests/tabN/**`, using their scoped `TabApi`. `api.datasets(subtab_id)` enumerates the read contracts; `api.query(subtab_id, DataRequest(...))` returns recursively immutable bounded data. `DataRequest` continuation pages require the prior `data_hash`. Advanced states and open policies must remain explicit, not filled with fabricated market results.
- Tab 3 may add **`src/sfm_fx_new_age/tabs/tab3/backend.py` with `install(api)`**. It is discovered by the headless production loader independently of Qt widget imports. Keep this module Qt-neutral; it must not acquire runtime/database/connector/order handles.
- The install-time analytical capability permits `register_parameter(ParameterDefinition)`, `register_surface(MutableSurface)`, `register_variable(node_id, VariableDefinition)`, `register_producer(node_id, handler, method_version=..., source_ref=..., trigger_frames=...)`, and `register_recipe(Recipe, composer)`. Registration closes after startup. Existing core producer authority cannot be replaced; sensor registrations must target source-declared sensor nodes.
- Producer callbacks receive frozen declared-frame inputs, frozen dependency state/output and their scoped prior objects. Return `AnalyticalResult` containing bounded JSON-compatible values, typed immutable objects and/or owned `VariableUpdate` observations. Variable definitions require pair/timeframe ownership; future/cross-scope/cross-owner publications are rejected. Recipe callbacks also receive a frozen scoped analytical-object snapshot and may return only bounded typed market candidates, never execution commands. These are trusted-code capabilities, not a Python sandbox.
- Shared recipe composition/caching and object publication remain core-owned. Use `services.geometry` and the immutable contract DTOs rather than copying ATR, confidence, rule or execution engines into widgets. The installed-file discovery test proves a lane-owned backend module actually executes through the production loader.
- Tab 4 local binding: `api.command('4.2', 'BIND_RESEARCH_CANDIDATE', {'candidate_id': ..., 'candidate_revision': ..., 'variant_id': ..., 'units': '...', 'expected_version': ..., 'user_initiated': True})`. This queues a source-hash-bound research-only local signal and returns its identity; it cannot contact a broker.
- Tab 4 risk detail: query `issue_preview` with `object_id=signal_id`, or use `CONFIRM_SELECTED_SIGNAL` with the current signal revision. Preview is no-write. Only the existing `4.1 / ISSUE` command with deliberate human initiation and visible research acknowledgement can request a pending Limit order.
- Tab 4 history: `REFRESH_HISTORY` with `order_id` reconciles one known local order; with both `start` and `end` it requests read-only native order/deal history. Do not mix those forms. Read `broker_history_jobs`/`broker_history` on 4.8 and `broker_pending` on 4.3; inspect completion/last-known metadata before making absence claims.
- Each lane stays inside the ownership map above. Shared schema/service changes return to the coordinator for serialized integration; no lane forks a feed/calculation/store/gateway or edits another lane. No worker writes a separate diary or changes this file concurrently.

#### Verified acceptance and artifacts

- Persistent offline checkpoint: 126 fixture bars, exactly one fixture submission, duplicate rejection, cross-tab order identity, UNKNOWN → restart → reconciliation, and local cancellation preserving the pending order. **Zero cTrader network calls or real broker orders** in that checkpoint.
- Actual SDK probe ran in a separate process without network. Protocol tests used recording workers/synthetic messages, not real authenticated account data. Local OAuth callback HTTP lifecycle was exercised on loopback and the listener closed; no cTrader login occurred.
- Final wheel built and installed outside the source import path. All **95 dataset bindings** and offline recovery passed under both the project interpreter with the optional SDK and the minimal system interpreter without it; neither headless probe imported Qt. All **73 packaged files** matched the final source bytes exactly.

An initial probe incorrectly expected closing an externally owned view to close its core; the corrected probe preserves that ownership boundary and then explicitly closes its core. No lifecycle policy was weakened to satisfy the probe.

Desktop/menu validation and `metadata::trusted: true` readback passed. The scrolling secondary strip still has ordinary overflow; the full-name selector exposes the exact subtab names. Professional navigation polish remains UI-lane work, not a shared-backend blocker.

#### Scope and actual changes

- Removed the global top forex-pair picker and W1/D1/H4/H1 text. The four full-name main tabs occupy that primary navigation area directly beneath the compact brand/account header. Pair selection remains available in Forex Pair Watchlist through the existing shared command; no feed, symbol or timeframe contract was deleted.
- Added `MainTabDeck` while retaining the existing nested page/index/plugin contracts. Removed the redundant visible secondary tab strip. One full-name View selector, explicit `View n of N`, Previous/Next controls, Ctrl+1…Ctrl+4, Ctrl+K and visible keyboard focus provide navigation. Each main tab retains its own selected view. No subtab was renamed or removed.
- Removed the persistent 44-row component table. `ScannerOverview` reads the existing shared scanner, shows an honest compact state summary and exposes one component at a time through its inspector. No-source startup explicitly says `No market data` and `44 not evaluated`; registered producers are not presented as finished algorithms. The backend retains all 44 nodes and 130 edges.
- Reduced generic-panel technical clutter; contract/owner detail is a tooltip rather than a permanent line. Watchlist selection is shown as `Active`, not raw True/False, and its status is `PAIR SELECTOR`, not an ambiguous implication that live data are available. The seven-row watchlist fits without inner scrolling at 960×640.
- Narrowed Signal Queue's visible columns to its six useful summary fields while preserving complete row data and shared Issue/preflight contracts. Research acknowledgement, Issue, Cancel signal and Cancel all unissued remain in view at 960×640, including after command feedback. Vertically shrinkable tables prevent height-for-width sizing from pushing the actions below the page.
- Initial fallback construction reads only the active `1.1` snapshot, not all 42 views. Visible content has change-fingerprint guards and a 500 ms refresh interval; core/event pumping has its separate 50 ms timer. All 42 widget containers are still constructed: this is not a claim of lazy widget instantiation or a separate background daemon.
- Made global theme installation idempotent. Reopening views no longer reinstalls the application style and repolishes all existing windows. The theme-signature guard also avoids relying on Qt stylesheet wrappers exposing the underlying Fusion style name.
- Architecture inspection repaired two legacy display-path inconsistencies: variable snapshots now use the causal, account/source-scoped catalogue adapter rather than raw database rows, and the quote-freshness snapshot uses `feed.quote_max_age_seconds`, matching the shared dataset adapter. Added regressions for account switching, old-epoch variable staleness and nondefault quote-age limits.
- Added `tests/test_gui_navigation_refinement.py`, `tests/test_architecture_review_regressions.py` and the new shared scanner widget; updated README navigation instructions. No connector, database schema, order service, machine registry, tab-lane plugin or account policy was changed in this batch.

Qt offscreen checks alone were not treated as visual acceptance.

To reach a complete product-level usability assessment, tab lanes still need their domain-specific charts/forms/editors, task-specific empty/error/disabled-action guidance, and real-user plus assistive-technology testing. Generic fallback tables are not finished professional workflows. No claim is made about unsupported sizes below the 960×640 minimum, high-DPI/accessibility certification, long-duration connected performance or validated strategy profitability.

### 2026-11-06 — Chart Engine, contained Trading Tools and saved browser login accepted offline

#### User direction and implementation

- Renamed the canonical and visible Tab 1 title to **Chart Engine**. Promoted Tab 2 to **Trading Tools**; its former central switchboard, scanner catalogue/router and ten `2.*` contracts remain available internally through `runtime.api(2)` rather than appearing as user widgets. This deliberately changes presentation, not the stable 42 backend IDs or 41 routes. The supplied corpus/other SFM products/profiles remain untouched.
- Added a dark QMdiArea workspace with a registered-tool picker, open/focus, contained resize/minimize/maximize/close, Arrange and Close all. Tool state survives main-tab navigation. Each independently contributed tool receives a fresh scoped TabApi; closing releases only its API/subscriptions, never the shared core/connector. Lazy `TabPlugin(2).register_tool(...)` is the Tab 2 extension path; no dummy engineering tabs are retained in the operator GUI.
- Ported the supplied SFM FX Calculator .deb's pair groups and decimal P/L math into a native Qt widget rather than launching/reparenting the Tk app or installing its .deb. Supports BUY/SELL, 0.01–1.00 lots, pip/units/gross estimate, user-supplied currency conversion, per-pair memory and Reset pair. It is explicitly offline/manual, assumes a standard 100,000-unit lot, excludes costs and has no feed/credential/order side effects. Compact 960×640 layout shows all inputs, reset and results with no internal scrolling.
- Replaced the connection form with browser-first sign-in. The top-right button starts OAuth once application setup exists; the callback is consumed by the connector even if the dialog closes. Access/refresh tokens save automatically in the existing 0700-directory/0600-file atomic **plaintext** store, not an encrypted OS keychain. No cTrader password is requested or saved. A visibly checked startup-reconnect option defaults on for the first login; its wording accurately controls reconnect, not whether OAuth tokens exist. Sign out disables reconnect, removes account tokens, retains app setup and does not cancel broker orders or claim broker-side token revocation.
- Added generation-bound cancellation so late callbacks/refresh/start events cannot resurrect a signed-out or superseded login. Failed renewal, browser expiry/exchange failure and provider authorization invalidation require a fresh human sign-in without blind retry. Renewed tokens rotate in the private store; no secret enters SQLite, snapshots or logs.
- Remembered reconnect restores **only the exact previously selected broker/endpoint/account/origin scope after fresh native authentication**, then read-only quotes/history. A first browser login can auto-select one account only after both endpoint inventories and metadata complete; multiple accounts require user choice. Demo/live remains metadata, never an eligibility gate. No callback, reconnection, tool or research path creates/replays a broker order.
- Added one-time private app-profile import behind **Application setup**. Import validates local profile shape (`client_id`, loopback `redirect_uri`, app credential and optional scope); copies only the app credential into this application's private store; discards foreign account tokens and rejects unsafe permissions/symlinks or oversized credential files. Import starts no network and does not prove provider registration. It cannot invent a registered provider client identity.

The provider initially returned `Malformed client_id parameter`. Application Client ID, application/page number, cTrader login and email are distinct; no value was guessed from the page number.
- Retrieved the actual Client ID from the user's already authenticated, exact registered-application Sandbox and matched it to the application settings. The supplied Refresh token matched the Sandbox token, but the supplied application secret did not match the registered app. Corrected the application pair using that same registration, without another app registration, account-password entry, cookie/profile extraction or cross-product credential reuse. Secret values are intentionally absent from this diary.
- Authoritative token renewal succeeded and saved server expiry plus rotated tokens. Real application auth/account discovery found one authorized LIVE and one DEMO account. The human explicitly selected DEMO account ending **[REDACTED_ACCOUNT_SUFFIX]** in the account-source window; no default account class/eligibility restriction was added.
- Connected verification exposed three independent feed defects after authentication: SDK one-second batches triggered `BLOCKED_PAYLOAD_TYPE`; reconnect could seed history from previous-epoch persisted metadata before current native metadata arrived; independently updated bid/ask sides could temporarily cross and raise a whole-account disconnect. Each received a failing deterministic regression, a narrow repair and passing regression before another connected attempt.

#### Implemented changes

- Added masked one-time registered-application/session linking (`ui/session_link.py`, `IMPORT_CTRADER_SESSION`) through a private file reference; app-only import still ignores foreign account tokens. Existing registered playground redirects can renew their saved grant without falsely claiming SFM catches the portal callback. Loopback applications retain browser-first OAuth. No cTrader account password is requested or saved.
- Corrected token exchange to GET for authorization-code exchange and POST for refresh, following the official SDK. Added bounded nonsecret error classification, visible pre-account application failures, no blind credential retries and disabled startup retry for malformed Client ID. Restored immutable nested broker calendar tuples after SQLite/JSON reload.
- Paced actual SDK frames conservatively at one per 350 ms rather than five-frame one-second bursts, with monotonic spacing under delayed callbacks, independent ten-second heartbeats and per-connection queues cleared on loss. TLS hostname verification, SDK serialization, correlations and explicit-write allowlists remain intact. This is not an automatic retry of a failed broker operation.
- Initial history waits until native symbol specs, active authority and broker calendar provenance all match the current connection epoch. Persisted previous-source metadata cannot create a new-epoch job. Earlier failed/old-source jobs remain historical evidence, not current-feed acceptance.
- Incoherent crossed bid/ask combinations invalidate only that pair's executable quote; actual side prices/times remain unmodified. No clamped/fabricated quote or known-invalid old quote is published, and coherent subsequent native sides recover without disconnecting unrelated symbols. Malformed unknown frames retain fail-closed handling.
- Added `scripts/verify_ctrader_feed.py`: bounded real read-only SDK fence, explicit account selection, current-epoch freshness/history checks, no-write audit and redacted receipt. Added `tests/test_registered_application_link.py`, `test_sdk_transport_pacing.py`, `test_feed_reconnect_epoch.py`, `test_quote_side_coherence.py` and expanded existing connection/UI tests. ### 2026-11-07 — Weekend demo reconnect, native chart controls and closed-market acceptance

#### Root cause and real endpoint diagnosis

- Time checked: **Saturday 2026-11-07**, host GMT/UTC. The user reported that DEMO seemed inaccessible while LIVE worked and requested chart usability/depth plus feed repair. Continued this checkout only; no cBots, Windows VM, unrelated SFM product, subagent or additional progress notebook.
- `.runtime/weekend-feed-diagnosis.json` observed application/auth/account responses on **both** endpoints. The demo session then dropped on a local `ProtoOASpotEvent` `ValueError`; LIVE remained available. This was not an observed provider denial or a demo-only weekend eligibility rule.
- Reproduced with a failing source-native regression: `MarketService.set_quote()` compared the new connection's last-market snapshot against the prior epoch's final quote. The official weekend snapshot can be older, producing `Out-of-order quote rejected`; `ConnectorService.poll()` classified that exception as a native frame failure and disconnected the DEMO account. Fixed ordering comparison to the same epoch only, preserving the provider's old timestamp and stale state. Same-epoch old prices remain rejected; reconnect never manufactures current quote freshness.
- Real diagnosis also showed historical count responses extending before the requested interval; prior pagination walked back as far as 2017 and consumed its timeout despite already receiving the requested bars. `_backfill_page()` now stops at the requested lower bound, respects an explicit `hasMore` response when available, and enforces backward cursor progress / 64-page budget. Out-of-range rows are not ingested, rejected jobs are not blindly retried, and interval exhaustion is not conflated with completeness of unrelated older history.
- `.runtime/weekend-feed-repaired.json`: both DEMO and LIVE returned seven same-connection last-price snapshots and **28/28** complete history jobs / **4,564** bars each, with **no connector errors or request/disconnect failures**. Read/auth-only worker fences remained active. Saved human account preference was restored after diagnostic comparisons; the GUI was not silently switched to live.

#### Chart and shared-data changes

- Extracted source-bound interaction/rendering into `ui/chart_canvas.py`; retained `CandleCanvas` import compatibility from `ui/chart_workspace.py`. The shared 4/42 registry, 10/10/13/9 identities, shared connector/persistence/scanner/order gateway and human-only Issue boundary are unchanged.
- Added visible zoom +/−, Latest/follow and Fit, pointer-centered wheel zoom, arrow/Home/End/+− navigation, manual price-scale dragging and reset, bounded UTC date navigation, and Candles/OHLC/Line types. New bars preserve the inspected right-edge candle when panned back. Pair/frame view and local horizontal levels are remembered under full account/epoch/symbol/frame identity; a different source does not inherit drawings or forming data. Drawings are in-memory display notes, not persistent orders or broker levels.
- Added ruler measurement of signed pips/percent, bar separation and elapsed time through Tools or Shift+drag; horizontal levels, grid/crosshair controls and clear drawings. Escape returns to Pan and synchronizes the toolbar. Crosshair price/time tags and OHLC inspection remain read-only. Export actions were actually exercised through local destination dialogs: exact observed closed-bar CSV and PNG bytes were verified, not merely helper output.
- Added a **shared core-owned cached** `services/chart_studies.py` service for causal trailing SMA20/EMA50, with explicit warm-up and a bounded 64-result cache. Data comes through scoped `TabApi` chart queries, including bounded study arrays on paginated reads; Qt widgets do not compute/publish strategy indicators or fork market ownership. Color-matched legends distinguish studies; they are display-only, not accepted strategy signals.
- Added backward-compatible optional `Bar.tick_volume` (unknown by default, nonnegative native integer when observed), mapped from actual `ProtoOATrendbar.volume`. The chart renders native tick counts, explicitly not exchange volume, and never substitutes zero for missing data. Tick-built partial forming observations are separate, dashed, source/frame-bound, open-session-only and excluded from completed-bar counts / closed-bar studies.
- Added source-current chart health: authentication/account class, history state/failure, native session status, next scheduled opening and last completed bar. **BROKER DATA** now means provenance, not freshness; old prices are clearly **last market snapshot** during closure. A visible **Connection…** action opens the existing shared connection manager rather than instructing an already authenticated user to find a nonexistent Connect control. Connection management also reports DEMO/LIVE endpoint status independently.
- Added `SessionCalendar.next_open()` using provider weekly windows/timezone and known closure dates, including DST. Only current-epoch native calendar evidence can establish market closure. The observed schedule lists **Sunday 2026-11-08 22:01 UTC** as the next opening for these pairs; it is a schedule observation, not a holiday/maintenance guarantee.
- Added explicit verifier option `--allow-market-closed`: `PASS_CLOSED_MARKET_SNAPSHOTS_AND_HISTORY` requires seven current-epoch nonfuture snapshots, all 28 complete histories and seven source-current native calendars explicitly closed. Default fresh-quote acceptance is unchanged. Unknown/open calendars, old-epoch/future quotes, missing histories and connector errors cannot pass this alternative.

### 2026-11-07 — Official cTrader GUI research, chart interaction upgrade and native closed-bar path

#### Research scope and reference pictures

- User requested online research of cTrader GUI specifications and interaction pictures to improve the chart mechanics/data flow. Worked directly in this repository; no subagents, extra product checkout, cBot/Windows mutation, new registration, scheduled jobs or broker execution. Checked live UTC/local time before the market/session investigation.
- Retrieved **17 successful official Windows/Web/Open API pages**, covering chart toolbar/types/periods, chart opening/navigation, mouse/hotkeys, viewing options, drawing/editing/object management, chart layouts, linked symbols, Chartshot, Web settings/context menu, historical/live symbol data, FAQ, message contracts and rate limits. The two platform chart-toolbar guides and symbol-data guide anchor the comparison.[1][6][10]
- The API overview documents separate per-connection request limits: 50 nonhistorical and 5 historical requests per second. Existing conservative paced SDK dispatch remains the network boundary; this upgrade does not make widgets broker clients.[13]
- Heuristic self-review: **8/10**, a design judgment rather than measured user-study acceptance. Direct controls, undo, exact-value/source feedback and compact layouts improved; finite history, memory-only annotations/templates and incomplete advanced chart families remain reasons not to claim 10/10 or full parity.
- These are published behavioural specifications and illustrations, **not** an obtained proprietary internal GUI/pixel specification or a guarantee that all screenshots depict the current release. Two attempted Windows URLs returned HTTP errors; discovered supported Windows navigation links and used the official Web context-menu page rather than inventing missing Windows documentation.[17]
- Retained **109 distinct official reference pictures**, all successfully downloaded, URL/page-bound and SHA-256 recorded in `.runtime/ctrader-gui-research/picture-inventory.json`. Ten generated contact sheets preserve these original pictures; representative toolbar, drawings and viewing/layout sheets were visually inspected. Retrieval is not a claim that every picture was individually visually reviewed. Original-size PNGs are local research evidence, not redistributed product assets. Source text, retrieval inventory and citation/evidence ledger are alongside them.
- Relevant visual findings: compact grouped chart toolbar, right/bottom scales, named chart-object inventory, selected-object handles, editable properties and independent viewing switches.[1][3][5]
- Layout arrangement and linked-symbol groups have separate documented behaviour; symbol linking is not evidence of linked scrolling or crosshair.[14][15]
- Shortcut conflict is explicit: the Windows Open-charts page lists Ctrl+A for Ask, whereas its Hotkeys page assigns Ctrl+A to drawings and Ctrl+K to Ask. Web chart-type guidance also differs from the Windows hotkey map. Used Windows Alt+1/2/3/4 for chart type and preserved SFM's existing Ctrl+1…4 main sections and Ctrl+K pair selector; Ask remains an explicit menu switch. No claim of complete shortcut parity.[2][4][6]

#### Source-backed comparison and implemented/deferred scope

| Interaction family | Official reference | SFM result in this pass |
|---|---|---|
| Chart types and visible zoom | Standard OHLC/candles/line/dot, toolbar +/- and type selector.[1][6] | Added Dot; kept OHLC/Candles/Line; context menu and Alt+1…4 synchronize the visible selector. |
| Mouse wheel and chart navigation | Web settings offer Zoom/Scroll; Windows documents Ctrl+wheel, middle crosshair and fast page navigation.[4][9] | Configurable wheel mode, Ctrl+wheel always zooms, two-axis pan, time-axis drag scaling, middle-click crosshair, two-bar Left/Right, Page Up/Down, existing Home/End/+/- and pointer-anchored zoom. |
| Timeframe selection | Standard/tick/renko/range periods and favourites are distinct supported chart families.[1] | Up/Down reuses existing W1/D1/H4/H1 identities or Overview context. No extra pages/timeframes, synthetic renko/range/ticks or unsupported history feed. |
| Crosshair/snapshot and ruler | Price/time axis tags and historical OHLC/volume inspection; held drag measures price/time.[3] | Retained source-bound ruler and OHLC/V; hover now also gives active shared-study values. Empty right-margin/sparse space is **no completed candle**, not a clamped fictitious nearest-bar observation. |
| Drawing construction/editing | Horizontal/vertical, trend/ray, shapes/Fibonacci, object handles and property management.[3][8] | Six local kinds: Level, Vertical, Trend, Ray, Rectangle and Fibonacci retracement; timestamp/price anchors, nearby OHLC snap with Alt bypass, direct selection, whole-object/endpoint drag, deletion, lock/hide, colour and exact horizontal-price edit. |
| Object management and visibility | Object inventory, lock/hide/delete and show/hide-all controls.[1][3] | Visible Objects button plus Ctrl+D, local manager, Ctrl+Z/Y undo/redo, Ctrl+H hide-all and Del. Bounded to 100 objects/64 undo checkpoints per bounded source-view memory. No cloud/cross-symbol propagation. |
| Bid/ask, grid and tick volume | Viewing options distinguish quote sides, price lines, grid and native tick volume.[5][7] | Independent Bid/Ask switches with same-account/epoch/symbol validation, stale snapshot state, C/B/A axis markers, coalesced equal values, value-ordered collision layout and exact-price leader lines. Tick-volume pane now shows a numeric maximum; hover retains exact native V. |
| Studies | cTrader exposes a configurable indicator and panel/object workflow.[1][6] | Existing shared causal SMA20/EMA50 retained; did not pretend to implement cTrader's entire catalogue, parameter UI or multi-panel indicator system. |
| Layouts, linking and templates | Multi/single/free-chart modes, cloud workspace/templates and colour-linked symbol groups are separate features.[1][14][15] | Kept fixed four workspaces and 42 backend identities. Existing pair views stay linked to the one selected pair; no free/detached panes, cloud templates or inferred synchronized crosshair/scroll. |
| Export | Chartshot is an image-sharing/export feature, separate from chart observations.[16] | Retained local CSV of exact observations and local PNG. No external Chartshot upload. |
| History/live flow | Native live trendbars follow spot subscription and deliver the last closed bar in SpotEvent.trendbar.[10][12] | Implemented acknowledgement-ordered native stream ingress after bounded history, into the shared causal store rather than widget-owned feeds. |
| Order-related chart actions | cTrader's context menu also contains order/alert entry.[17] | **Intentionally excluded** from the new chart context menu. Existing human-only Issue gateway and account-neutral policy are unchanged. |
| Countdown/alerts/advanced analysis | Toolbar countdown and extensive drawing families are documented.[1][3] | Deferred countdown, alerts, freehand/text/channels/Gann/pitchfork/Fib fan-expansion and persistent local templates. They are not implied by this acceptance. |

#### Code and data-flow ownership

- New `ui/chart_objects.py` is a bounded local DrawingBook, with no broker/runtime/router/storage dependency. `chart_interactions.py` owns direct gestures/context menu/render geometry; `chart_object_manager.py` owns the dialog; `chart_axis.py` owns deterministic price-label placement. `chart_canvas.py` and `chart_workspace.py` integrate these without splitting shared market ownership. `app.py` routes period navigation into the existing dedicated views.
- Every annotation retains timestamps/prices, not reconstructed pixels. Books, undo state and viewport memory are isolated under full account/source-epoch/symbol/frame identity. A different connection cannot inherit drawings; an open manager rejects itself if its source changes. Drawings are memory-only and are **not** persisted across application restart, promoted to research levels/signals, used as order geometry or synchronized to cTrader.
- Actual code inspection found `ProtoOASubscribeLiveTrendbarReq` in the SDK read allowlist but **no producer sending those subscriptions**. The prior chart could bootstrap history and observe bid ticks, but the official native closed-bar path was not implemented. cTrader documents spots-before-live-bars and native last-closed data in the trendbar field.[10]
- Added `ConnectorService._subscribe_closed_bars()`: wait for source-current metadata, actual spot ACK and the relevant complete bounded history job; subscribe once per known symbol/frame in the selected account. Track **PENDING / ACTIVE / FAILED**, use the existing paced SDK read boundary, fail explicitly on error/timeout and never silently retry a failed subscription.
- Added `_closed_native_bar()`: accept only selected-account/current-epoch/current-calendar supported subscribed periods with close <= provider event time <= receive time. Preserve native OHLC/tick-volume, reject malformed/future/incomplete/other-source bars, deduplicate identical closes, ignore older provider updates and create a new causal immutable revision for a changed completed observation. Queries/studies/downstream events consume the same MarketService records.
- Canonical tick observations still create clearly partial forming data; connector calls `TickTimeline.ingest(finalize=False)` so a tick finalizer cannot race the native OHLC/volume owner. Other offline/tick workflows retain the existing default. Added watchlist scope/epoch metadata so the canvas can independently refuse a mismatched price line.
- The API FAQ explicitly says closed markets can produce only an initial last-price spot and that bars are not created without ticks. Therefore **ACTIVE subscriptions are acknowledgement proof, not actual new-bar delivery proof**.[11] Current closed-market `native_live_bars_received` remains **0**; no invented event was injected into the real path.

### 2026-11-07 — Cross-workspace workflows, scoped persistence and targeted refresh accepted

**Authorization and boundaries.** Implemented the user's “implement all suggestions” instruction for the audited tab/cross-tab improvements. Preserved four workspaces, all 42 identities split **10/10/13/9**, internal Trading Tools contracts, operator-owned account selection, one connector and one human-only **Issue** path. No subagents were used. `README.md` now documents the actual controls and limitations; this file remains the sole project diary.

#### Implemented scope

1. Added core-owned `services/workflow.py` with inspection envelopes binding account namespace, source epoch, symbol, timeframe, observation time, object identity/revision/content hash and isolated replay origin. Scoped `TabApi` consumers cannot forge or reuse changed-source objects. Explicit navigation and Return do not acknowledge research, bind candidates or send broker commands. Destination permissions are checked before navigation writes/emission; internal `2.*` engineering contracts are not operator navigation targets.
2. Registered shared real inspectors in all Tab 3 views and applicable Tab 4 views through their existing plugin ownership. Added matching Chart/Research links, record selection, bounded paging and record detail. Generic chart context remains distinct from a selected research object. Broker/local-order records cannot enter Research or calculator datasets.
3. Added causal chart regions and detailed evidence inspection. Regions start at availability time, refuse another source and do not appear in an older panned window before they were known. Historical chart selections withhold current-only broker extras and quotes. Readiness explicitly reports missing frames, unavailable methods, disabled/evaluated states and candidates; unavailable does not mean a scientifically established no-setup result.
4. Replaced the repeated signal-table presentation at `4.2` with candidate/signal/evidence detail, explicit variant and units, core-owned local proposal binding, and no-write preview/validation. Proposal binding never sends. The Issue queue still requires its own deliberate human acknowledgement and separate **Issue** action; changed selection or same-ID revision resets acknowledgement. Compact detail/preview tabs, hidden inapplicable variant controls and concise post-validation feedback keep actions onscreen without reducing font size.
5. Passed a tool-owned scoped API into the contained calculator. **Use chart context**, **Replace scenario prices**, **Use broker spec** and **Use observed conversion** are distinct opt-in actions. Manual edits remain authoritative; historical cutoffs receive no later quote. Provider snapshot times and direct/inverse conversion provenance remain explicit. Closed tools release their APIs without closing the runtime; late MDI destruction signals are guarded.
6. Connected Chart Replay and Research Replay to the existing immutable datasets/run clock with Play/Pause/Step/Seek and causal availability. Replay contexts remain within replay-aware destinations; no current market research is shown under a replay label. Replay is not execution or profitability proof.
7. Added distinct read-only chart visuals for local order records, native pending inventory and native positions, with broker identities, observation times and links to their owning Tab 4 records. Native normalization preserves symbol/side/units without adopting orders or manufacturing positions. A broker record is never implicitly selected merely because it was first inserted into a combo box.
8. Persisted bounded drawings, human notes and chart preferences through the one SQLite owner with payload allowlists and version checks. Reconciled the legacy note command with the same store. A new source epoch archives human annotations rather than transplanting them; only safe account/pair/frame display preferences carry forward. Undo stacks/pan offsets and rulers stay local/in-memory. Version conflicts retain local edits and require explicit reload.
9. Added explicit bounded earlier-history requests through the shared continuity/connector owner, current-epoch native metadata/calendar checks, deterministic job deduplication and retained failure/partial status. No widget-owned feed or blind retry was introduced.
10. Added domain-event dirty routing and visible-page refresh coalescing. Core/event pumping remains independent, with a five-second ageing/status fallback. `release-refresh-profile.json` compares equivalent short explicit fixture workloads against the tar-preserved pre-change source: **10 refresh calls, 20 dataset reads before, 0 after; 0 hidden-page reads**. This is an idle-work measurement, not a throughput/load/reliability claim.

### 2026-11-08 — Chart object interaction and settings accepted

**Scope.** Completed the requested deeper cTrader-like chart/object handling, using the retrieved official object/toolbar/view references as interaction inspiration, not a full-parity claim. Preserved the four workspaces, 42 stable identities (10/10/13/9), account-neutral policy, one connector/store/workflow owner and explicit human-only Issue. No subagents were used.

#### User-facing changes

- **Nine local drawing types:** horizontal level, vertical line, trend line, ray, rectangle, ellipse, triangle, Fibonacci and plain-text note. Tools groups lines/text, shapes, selection, measuring and object actions. The toolbar, chart context menu, Objects manager and keyboard provide redundant access rather than hidden-only commands.
- **Object manipulation:** multi-selection/selection box, handles, move, Ctrl-drag duplication, local copy/cut/paste, duplicate, hide/show, lock/unlock, find, price/colour, front/back, delete and undo/redo. Configure nudge sizes in bars/pips; Alt+arrows nudges and Shift+Alt applies ten steps. Enter/double-click opens precise properties; Ctrl+D opens Objects; Ctrl+H toggles drawings. Clipboard, active gesture and undo remain bound to exact source/account/epoch/symbol/frame.
- **Transactional properties:** name, colour, width/style, fill/opacity, label size/bold/visibility, per-object snapping, extensions, zoom-dependent view-angle labels, exact UTC/price anchors, multiline text and custom Fibonacci percentages/presets. Untouched coordinates/style values preserve original precision; locked anchors cannot move. Apply commits validated local state, OK applies/closes, Cancel discards only unapplied edits. Stale/concurrently changed objects are refused rather than overwritten.
- **Future defaults and bulk appearance:** per-tool defaults are editable without creating an object and survive SQLite reopening. They affect new objects only. Bulk style fields are individually opt-in; actual dialog checks compare coordinates, names, locking and visibility before/after. Dedicated actions remain separate and undoable.
- **Chart Settings:** Appearance, Navigation, Visibility and Help tabs, with colour presets, candle/wick/line/dot/axis sizes, bull/bear fill, grid directions/style, crosshair style, margins/padding, wheel/drag behavior, snap/hit distances, configured nudge steps and drawing/title/study/research/broker/volume/bid/ask visibility. Scroll-contained forms keep Apply/Cancel/OK reachable at compact size.
- **Blank-space honesty:** drawings can use future chart space without inventing candles or OHLC. Time coordinates beyond loaded bars use explicitly nominal timeframe spacing, not a forecast broker calendar. Price snapping uses observed OHLC only; ruler inspection requires an observed candle. Trend/ray angles are view angles that change with zoom, not immutable market geometry.

The declared `SEPARATE_BROKER_CANCEL` command is reachable through scoped TabApi, not a GUI button. Nonhuman cancellation is rejected; local Cancel all unissued leaves the pending order intact. Duplicate cancellation is rejected without another send. GUI cancellation remains **BLOCKED_NO_CANCEL_CONTROL**.

Parsed the generated SDK Limit protobuf and exact account/order cancel payload. Each recorded lifecycle sends one New Limit and one Cancel request, waits in **UNKNOWN** until injected acknowledgements, and reaches **CANCELED**. Duplicate/late acknowledgements cannot regress state; no position is fabricated. These labels and injected native events are test data, not real endpoint connections or broker proof.

### 2026-11-08 — Tab 1 chart visuals, axis settings and dedicated rulers completed

**Verdict: PASS for the requested display-only chart enhancement.** The new `ui/chart_axes.py` mixin gives Chart Engine separate responsive price/time gutters, adaptive UTC labels, font-aware price ticks and collision spacing, optional axis panels/minor ticks and hover highlighting. The existing navy/slate appearance and the four-workspace/42-backend-identity **10/10/13/9** contract are preserved. No account restriction, connector ownership change or broker action was introduced.

#### Operator changes

- The **Measure** menu provides **Range ruler**, **Time ruler** and **Price ruler**; its button shows the active measurement mode without duplicating that label under Tools. Drag in the plot or along the corresponding axis. Ordinary axis dragging still scales; **Shift+drag** an axis measures only that dimension. Esc/clear actions remove the temporary measurement.
- Time rulers display observed candle intervals, actual signed elapsed UTC time and exact loaded-bar endpoint timestamps, with gaps included rather than an invented trading calendar. A shaded interval, bracket, connector guides and non-overlapping **T1/T2** badges make the span inspectable. Price rulers show signed pips, percentage, exact **P1/P2** endpoints and connected price-rail badges. They support reversed and zero-bar measurements, including JPY pip scaling.
- **Settings → Axes / rulers** adds price/time label spacing, explicit **Adaptive UTC / ISO date + time / Date only**, axis text size, rail/minor tick/highlight controls, measurement colour, guide/readout switches and four readout corners. Axis context menus expose reset, measurement, clear and settings. Apply saves; Cancel retains applied changes and discards only later unapplied edits.
- Measurements are transient and require loaded source-bound bars. Empty/future chart space never fabricates candles; source/frame changes clear the ruler. Negative panned price ranges refuse invalid measurements safely. A time-only ruler cannot silently become a price scenario in a calculator handoff. Rulers do not create drawings, observations, signals or broker commands, and do not change pan/scale.
- `README.md` now documents the controls and boundaries. Compact layouts intentionally allow scrolling inside the settings dialog while keeping Apply/Cancel available. At **960×640**, a persistent readout can still obscure a shallow plot: move it to another corner or hide it. Heuristic compact visual assessment is **8/10**, not a test metric; a future chart-focus layout would offer more plotting area without changing measurement semantics. No claim of complete cTrader parity is made.

### 2026-11-09 — Public forex strategy catalogue and projection switches completed

**Verdict: PASS for the finite 32-profile public-method catalogue.** Classification follows the user's correction: a method with a public online basis is non-custom for this catalogue, regardless of an SFM prefix or local stable ID. Public order blocks retain `sfm.order_block.research.v1`. This release does not claim to enumerate every strategy, discretionary interpretation or parameter variation on the internet. Custom SFM-only execution/capital/reabsorption/redistribution/treasury/SLtoTP combinations are not imported; existing candidate workflows and historical objects are preserved.

#### Delivered operator and analytical paths

- **Tab 3 → Strategy and Recipe Library (3.1)** has 32 individually effective tick options, search, a frame selector, exact-rule/source/status details and **Open pair chart**. Named harmonic AB=CD, Gartley, Bat and Butterfly and Elliott impulse/ABC hypotheses each have separate switches. The full roster is in `README.md` and `contracts/public_strategies.py`; it covers trend, momentum, mean reversion, breakout, candlestick, structural, pattern and level methods. The existing independent D1/H4 candidate switch remains reachable in **Trade Setup Builder (3.5)**.
- The real path is widget → existing strict `ENABLE_RECIPE` command → versioned persisted state → core-owned `PublicProjectionService` → closed-bar algorithm → immutable scoped snapshot → Tab 1 geometry. No widget owns feeds/calculations, no recipe issues orders, and no generic test-zone producer replaces a named detector. The existing typed order-block producer retains sole ownership of its zones; the new wrapper does not duplicate it.
- `public_features.py` caches shared close-based SMA/EMA/MACD/RSI/stochastic/ADX/Bollinger/Donchian/Ichimoku math per source/frame evaluation. `public_algorithms.py` codes the declared price/pivot/range/ratio rules and causal availability. Source scope, epoch, pair, frame, method/version, effective profile and closed-bar inputs bind cache and snapshot identities. Own-frame results cannot migrate to another chart; explicitly labelled D1/W1 reference levels preserve their source frame.
- Warm-up, unavailable prerequisites, evaluated no-setup and ready output are distinct. Checkboxes stop their own projection production, not just painting. Clearing one also removes its layer from inspected historical charts; immutable disabled revisions remain available for explicit audit rather than resurfacing through a navigation link. Source changes clear geometry; later bar corrections preserve earlier as-of snapshots. Replay never borrows current public layers. Identical-input recovery reuses the prior recorded revision without changing its observation time.
- Price lines/zones/markers use chart palette settings and the shared collision-managed price-caption rail. Oscillators remain metrics rather than nonsensical price-axis lines. The calculation budget is 2,048 completed bars per method/frame; output budgets are 256 samples per series, 32 markers and 16 zones per method, plus at most 12 public price captions. These are bounded projection profiles, not exhaustive historical pattern enumerators or an automatic AND/OR trade qualifier.
- Elliott is an explicit confirmed-pivot price skeleton, not a verified recursive wave count; Wyckoff is an OHLC range/event hypothesis, not observed institutional volume; harmonic ratio conventions and tolerances are disclosed. No genuine volume/depth/COT/fundamental data is manufactured. The public source map contains **27** unique references: **24** directly retrieved pages and **3** primary-source Elliott search excerpts whose direct pages denied HTTP 403. Search-only evidence is not misrepresented as fetched article bodies.

#### Implemented behavior and boundaries

### V21 — Host-aware CPU projection and post-change requalification

**Adaptive multicore projection is implemented and qualified. This is not an unconditional end-to-end 100% claim.** The accepted six-pair/four-frame, four-workspace, 42-view, 83-analytical-profile and 91-requirement scope remains unchanged. The principal measured caveat is still owner-loop responsiveness under startup/feed load; analytical concurrency does not turn 83 profiles into autonomous trading systems.

- **Host-aware defaults:** ordinary Desktop startup now defaults to `--compute-workers auto`; this host exposes 16 logical CPUs in its affinity set and no finite cgroup CPU quota, giving a 15-worker ceiling after one owner scheduling slot is removed from the calculation budget. Capacity also considers available memory at a 384 MiB-per-worker budget and the fixed 24-lane workload ceiling. Workers start only on demand. A positive CLI integer can lower the ceiling, and `1` stays serial. No host affinity, quota, nice setting or external service was changed. The connection status dialog exposes active/coalesced work and failures.
- **Authority and lifetime:** spawned workers receive bounded JSON-compatible immutable computation snapshots, not database, Qt, account/session, connector, SDK, credentials or broker handles. Only exact shipped pure evaluators are eligible; custom handlers remain serial. Queued jobs are latest-only per pair/frame, in-flight work is bounded, and owner-thread drain rechecks job/source/epoch identity, current switches and expiry before persistence. Pending or failed calculation cannot expose old built-in projections as current. Source invalidation cancels admission of old results; shutdown cancels queued work and joins the children. Protected order, qualification/binding, strategy definitions, study and architecture owners retain their predecessor bytes.
- **Avoided duplicate owner work:** no-output display recipes now share one immutable composition snapshot per refresh, with invalidation after a real publication changes prior objects. The pre/post original offline contexts retain exact equality for 327 analytical snapshots, 5,584 immutable strategy revisions and 4,648 current projection records. This saved-state equivalence is not native source proof or a general speedup claim. Raw profiles are retained; profiler-instrumented nested Qt/thread cumulative timings must not be treated as independent uninstrumented wall-time phases.
- **Complete post-change test estate:** 70 modules and **1,232 unique passing cases per build** in source and isolated installation, zero failures/errors/skips, executed in nine bounded groups per build. All 1,222 predecessor test identities/outcomes and their original test bytes are preserved. The ten additional cases cover real spawned-worker/old-serial equality, quota/affinity/memory limits, coalescing, bounded work, owner-thread handling, idle/serial behavior, withdrawal/disabling/expiry/token refusal, shared freezing and joined shutdown. This remains complete-estate grouped execution, not a claimed single-process pytest pass.
- **Genuine native calculation equivalence:** captured all 24 first native pair/frame process jobs from the normal installed saved-session path. **All 1,992 projection bodies** exactly match the pre-existing serial owner implementation in both source and isolated installation. This is source-bound replay of original native inputs; it does not manufacture market setups or replace fresh native observations with fixtures.
- **Native package/operational checks:** rebuilt wheel and isolated payload contain 154 matching product files. Source and installed ordinary X11 saved-session/research journeys passed; source elapsed 92.27 seconds and installed elapsed 182.06 seconds, with four worker PIDs observed in each. A new-wheel clean Debian 13 X11/research run passed with no network or checkout, using the independently pinned previously built Debian runtime base. A real 2-CPU cgroup correctly selected serial calculation after owner reserve. Existing Desktop `--check`, five real launcher flag controls and no-pyc compilation of 223 Python files passed.
- **Sustained active-pool observation:** the installed ordinary X11 run completed **902.71 seconds** against disposable state with all 83 analytical switches enabled there. It observed four calculation worker PIDs, 2,944 submitted jobs, zero worker errors, maximum eight in-flight jobs, zero final queued/in-flight/pending lanes, unchanged normal database records, no fixture imports, zero broker writes/mutations, stopped SDK and no owned-thread residue. Three outstanding bounded jobs were joined/discarded at close rather than committed after shutdown; completed/accepted counters are not presented as submitted-job totals. All task-owned processes were terminal before acceptance.
- **Remaining caveat, not hidden by PASS:** maximum uninstrumented heartbeat delay was **20.81 seconds** in the sustained run (the shorter installed run observed 22.83 seconds). The core-owned ingestion/persistence/rendering path remains serial; more calculation workers alone cannot establish a stall-free UI, strict latency SLA or indefinite endurance. This remains the next engineering performance priority. Historical studies remain the explicitly bounded 14 price-marker models, not 83 independently executable/backtested systems. New Issue remains a human action; no new order was used for qualification, and future provider/broker availability and profitability are not guaranteed.
- **Evidence and retention:** `.runtime/multicore-v21/acceptance-matrix.json` records the bounded pass and explicitly retains the responsiveness caveat and scope limits. Raw red/focused attempts, setup/reader refusals, source snapshots, wheel/install/container identities, complete suites, native originals and measured runs are preserved. Three terminal task-owned profiling SQLite copies were retained as full-byte/hash-verified lossless archives before retiring only the uncompressed disposable copies. The manifest-last read-only seal and external `.runtime/multicore-final-readback-v21.json` govern final closure; this remains the sole progress diary.
